### Bias Amplification in RAG: Poisoning Knowledge Retrieval to Steer LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11415v1)
- **Authors**: Linlin Wang, Tianqing Zhu, Laiqiao Qin, Longxiang Gao, Wanlei Zhou
- **Abstract**: In Large Language Models, Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems can significantly enhance the performance of large language models by integrating external knowledge. However, RAG also introduces new security risks. Existing research focuses mainly on how poisoning attacks in RAG systems affect model output quality, overlooking their potential to amplify model biases. For example, when querying about domestic violence victims, a compromised RAG system might preferentially retrieve documents depicting women as victims, causing the model to generate outputs that perpetuate gender stereotypes even when the original query is gender neutral. To show the impact of the bias, this paper proposes a Bias Retrieval and Reward Attack (BRRA) framework, which systematically investigates attack pathways that amplify language model biases through a RAG system manipulation. We design an adversarial document generation method based on multi-objective reward functions, employ subspace projection techniques to manipulate retrieval results, and construct a cyclic feedback mechanism for continuous bias amplification. Experiments on multiple mainstream large language models demonstrate that BRRA attacks can significantly enhance model biases in dimensions. In addition, we explore a dual stage defense mechanism to effectively mitigate the impacts of the attack. This study reveals that poisoning attacks in RAG systems directly amplify model output biases and clarifies the relationship between RAG system security and model fairness. This novel potential attack indicates that we need to keep an eye on the fairness issues of the RAG system.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates a new attack vector on Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems: bias amplification. It proposes a framework called Bias Retrieval and Reward Attack (BRRA) that poisons the knowledge retrieval process in RAG to steer Large Language Models (LLMs) toward generating biased outputs. BRRA involves adversarial document generation optimized through reward functions, subspace projection techniques to manipulate retrieval results, and a cyclic feedback mechanism for continuous bias amplification. The paper demonstrates that BRRA can significantly enhance model biases across various dimensions (gender, race, age, disability), even when the original query is gender-neutral. It also proposes a dual-stage defense mechanism combining retrieval noise perturbation and fairness constraints during generation to mitigate the attack's impact.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strength:** The paper's novelty lies in its focus on bias amplification as a security vulnerability in RAG systems. While previous research has explored data poisoning attacks on RAG, the specific focus on _amplifying_ pre-existing biases, rather than solely injecting misinformation, is a significant contribution. Also, the use of a cyclic feedback mechanism to continuously update the poisoned knowledge base enhances the impact of biases.
*   **Weakness:** The individual components of BRRA (adversarial document generation, subspace projection, reward functions) are not entirely novel in themselves. However, their combination and application in the context of RAG bias amplification is what makes this work unique.

**Significance:**

*   **Strength:** The paper is highly significant because it highlights a subtle but crucial security risk in RAG systems. Bias amplification can have real-world consequences, perpetuating stereotypes and discriminatory behavior. The paper also provides a good understanding of the interaction between the model, retriever, and knowledge base when attempting to steer LLMs toward biased results. By identifying this attack vector, the paper contributes to a more comprehensive understanding of RAG security and fairness.
*   **Weakness:** The paper's experimental evaluation could benefit from a more in-depth comparison to existing data poisoning attacks. While the ablation study provides some insights, a direct comparison of attack success rates and defense effectiveness against other poisoning methods would further strengthen the argument for BRRA's superiority in bias amplification. The reliance on benchmarks like BBQ and StereoSet, while standard, also limits the ecological validity of the findings. There is the need to determine how BRRA would function on datasets in different domains.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper represents a valuable contribution to the growing field of RAG security and fairness. The identification of bias amplification as a distinct attack vector is an important step towards building more robust and ethical RAG systems. The technical aspects of the research are generally strong, although some components rely on established methods. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating the effectiveness of BRRA in amplifying biases across various dimensions and models.

The study clarifies the relation between RAG system security and model fairness, while introducing a potential novel attack. However, there is need for more discussion of the interaction between model and retriever. This attack reveals that we need to keep an eye on fairness issues of RAG systems.

**Score: 7**

I am assigning a score of 7, taking into account the novelty and significance of the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Stop learning it all to mitigate visual hallucination, Focus on the hallucination target
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11417v1)
- **Authors**: Dokyoon Yoon, Youngsook Song, Woomyong Park
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) frequently suffer from hallucination issues, generating information about objects that are not present in input images during vision-language tasks. These hallucinations particularly undermine model reliability in practical applications requiring accurate object identification. To address this challenge, we propose \mymethod,\ a preference learning approach that mitigates hallucinations by focusing on targeted areas where they occur. To implement this, we build a dataset containing hallucinated responses, correct responses, and target information (i.e., objects present in the images and the corresponding chunk positions in responses affected by hallucinations). By applying a preference learning method restricted to these specific targets, the model can filter out irrelevant signals and focus on correcting hallucinations. This allows the model to produce more factual responses by concentrating solely on relevant information. Experimental results demonstrate that \mymethod\ effectively reduces hallucinations across multiple vision hallucination tasks, improving the reliability and performance of MLLMs without diminishing overall performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of object-level hallucinations in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It proposes TL-DPO (Target-Learning Direct Preference Optimization), a preference learning approach that focuses on targeted areas in images and specific chunks in responses where hallucinations occur. The approach involves creating a dataset containing hallucinated responses, correct responses, and target information (object locations and affected text chunks). TL-DPO aims to filter out irrelevant signals and focus on correcting hallucinations by applying preference learning specifically to these targeted regions.  Experimental results on various vision hallucination tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of TL-DPO in reducing hallucinations and improving the reliability and performance of MLLMs without a general performance drop.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its targeted approach to hallucination mitigation. Unlike previous preference learning methods that focus on global features or entire responses, TL-DPO pinpoints the specific image regions and text chunks contributing to hallucinations. The idea of masking non-relevant areas and chunks is a valuable contribution. The TL-DPO loss function, combining target generation and target conditioning losses, seems novel.

*   **Significance:** Hallucinations are a significant impediment to the trustworthiness and real-world applicability of MLLMs. Reducing these hallucinations, as TL-DPO aims to do, is crucial. The paper's claim to improve reliability and performance *without* diminishing overall performance is particularly significant, as many hallucination mitigation techniques come at the cost of general capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Targeted Approach:** The core idea of focusing on the target area of hallucination is intuitive and well-motivated.
    *   **Dataset Construction:** The described procedure for creating a dataset with hallucinated, corrected responses, and target information is a significant contribution. The detail on incorporating bounding box information enhances the approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper uses a variety of benchmark datasets to evaluate the method's effectiveness across diverse tasks, which strengthens the empirical claims.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation experiments demonstrating the contribution of both target generation and target conditioning losses provide deeper insights.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper includes theoretical analysis (Lemmas and Theorems) to justify the target learning approach, offering a more rigorous foundation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Existing Datasets:** The method relies on the Visual Genome dataset, which has limitations. The quality of the generated data is only as good as the underlying VQA dataset.
    *   **ChatGPT Dependence for Labeling:** While ChatGPT is a powerful tool, using it for labeling can introduce biases or inaccuracies. The paper should more thoroughly discuss the potential impact and mitigation strategies.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** The described process of dataset construction (involving ChatGPT for error identification and correction) might be difficult to scale to much larger datasets or more complex scenarios.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While the paper mentions qualitative results, presenting a more in-depth analysis with specific examples and comparisons would be valuable.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the method proves robust and scalable, it could become a valuable tool for developers working on MLLMs for various applications. The idea of targeted preference learning could be extended to other areas of multimodal research.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper presents a novel and potentially impactful approach to a critical problem in MLLMs. The targeted nature of TL-DPO is a significant advancement compared to existing methods. The inclusion of theoretical justification and comprehensive experiments strengthens the paper. However, the reliance on ChatGPT for labeling, concerns about the scalability of the data construction process, and limited qualitative analysis temper the overall assessment. The improvement in hallucination mitigation is also significant, however it does have a small decline in certain comprehensive benchmark metrics. It could also have a larger scope of different types of hallucinations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Long-Context LLM Inference via KV Cache Clustering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11418v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Hu, Shengnan Wang, Yutong He, Ping Gong, Jiawei Yi, Juncheng Zhang, Youhui Bai, Renhai Chen, Gong Zhang, Cheng Li, Kun Yuan
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) with extended context windows have become increasingly prevalent for tackling complex tasks. However, the substantial Key-Value (KV) cache required for long-context LLMs poses significant deployment challenges. Existing approaches either discard potentially critical information needed for future generations or offer limited efficiency gains due to high computational overhead. In this paper, we introduce Chelsea, a simple yet effective framework for online KV cache clustering. Our approach is based on the observation that key states exhibit high similarity along the sequence dimension. To enable efficient clustering, we divide the sequence into chunks and propose Chunked Soft Matching, which employs an alternating partition strategy within each chunk and identifies clusters based on similarity. Chelsea then merges the KV cache within each cluster into a single centroid. Additionally, we provide a theoretical analysis of the computational complexity and the optimality of the intra-chunk partitioning strategy. Extensive experiments across various models and long-context benchmarks demonstrate that Chelsea achieves up to 80% reduction in KV cache memory usage while maintaining comparable model performance. Moreover, with minimal computational overhead, Chelsea accelerates the decoding stage of inference by up to 3.19$\times$ and reduces end-to-end latency by up to 2.72$\times$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Chelsea, a novel framework for efficient inference of Large Language Models (LLMs) with long contexts by focusing on Key-Value (KV) cache clustering. Chelsea leverages the observation that key states exhibit high similarity along the sequence dimension.  It divides the sequence into chunks and employs a "Chunked Soft Matching" algorithm for identifying clusters based on similarity. The KV cache within each cluster is then merged into a single centroid, reducing memory usage and accelerating inference. The paper also provides a theoretical analysis of the computational complexity and optimality of their approach. Experimental results demonstrate significant KV cache memory reduction (up to 80%) and inference acceleration (up to 3.19x) with minimal impact on model performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical Problem Addressed:** Long-context LLMs are computationally expensive and pose deployment challenges due to large KV cache requirements. The paper directly tackles this important problem.
    * **Clear and Understandable Approach:** The "Chunked Soft Matching" algorithm and the overall Chelsea framework are relatively simple to understand and implement. This simplicity is a strength.
    * **Theoretical Justification:**  The paper provides theoretical analysis supporting the optimality of the intra-chunk partitioning strategy. This is a significant plus, adding rigor to the method.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of Chelsea in reducing memory usage and improving inference speed across various models and datasets. The performance compared to existing baselines like StreamingLLM, SnapKV, and CaM is a notable strength.
    * **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides insights into the importance of different components of Chelsea, particularly the chunking process and outlier head identification.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the framework is effective, the core idea of clustering and merging similar KV states is not entirely new.  KVMerger [14] also explores token similarity-based merging. Chelsea’s novelty lies primarily in its chunked approach and alternating partition strategy. The extension of Bipartite Soft Matching from Vision Transformers to KV cache compression, while novel in this context, builds upon existing work.
    * **Limited Generalizability (potential):**  The reported performance gains heavily rely on the specific long-context benchmarks (LongBench, NIAH) used. It's crucial to evaluate Chelsea on a wider range of tasks and data distributions to assess its robustness and general applicability, especially in real-world scenarios.
    * **Parameter Sensitivity:** The approach includes several hyperparameters (chunk size, outlier head ratio, compression ratio,  etc.).  The sensitivity of performance to these parameters and the effort required to tune them for different tasks is not fully addressed.  Ideally, a more adaptive or automatic parameter tuning mechanism would improve usability.
    * **Memory Offloading:**  As pointed out by the authors, focusing solely on GPU KV cache compression, without investigating memory offloading strategies, may be a limitation. Integrating memory offloading techniques could potentially yield even greater gains.
     * **Comparison in Zero-Shot Setting** Comparisons with the baselines are done in the zero-shot setting which is the strongest performance setting which favors the authors' approach of retaining original tokens. Performance in other settings where pretraining data may be used might not be as significant.

* **Significance:**
    * The paper is significant because it provides a practical and efficient solution to a critical problem in deploying long-context LLMs.
    * The theoretical analysis is a valuable contribution.
    * The strong experimental results suggest that Chelsea has the potential to significantly reduce the resource requirements of LLM inference, making them more accessible and deployable.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7/10**.

* **Rationale:**  The paper addresses a relevant and timely problem with a technically sound and empirically validated solution. The combination of chunking, alternating partition strategy, and theoretical justification adds value. However, the incremental nature of the core idea,  potential parameter sensitivity, and the limited evaluation on different datasets, prevent it from achieving a higher score. It's a solid contribution with practical implications, but further research is needed to fully explore its potential and limitations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PPDiff: Diffusing in Hybrid Sequence-Structure Space for Protein-Protein Complex Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11420v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenqiao Song, Tiaoxiao Li, Lei Li, Martin Renqiang Min
- **Abstract**: Designing protein-binding proteins with high affinity is critical in biomedical research and biotechnology. Despite recent advancements targeting specific proteins, the ability to create high-affinity binders for arbitrary protein targets on demand, without extensive rounds of wet-lab testing, remains a significant challenge. Here, we introduce PPDiff, a diffusion model to jointly design the sequence and structure of binders for arbitrary protein targets in a non-autoregressive manner. PPDiffbuilds upon our developed Sequence Structure Interleaving Network with Causal attention layers (SSINC), which integrates interleaved self-attention layers to capture global amino acid correlations, k-nearest neighbor (kNN) equivariant graph layers to model local interactions in three-dimensional (3D) space, and causal attention layers to simplify the intricate interdependencies within the protein sequence. To assess PPDiff, we curate PPBench, a general protein-protein complex dataset comprising 706,360 complexes from the Protein Data Bank (PDB). The model is pretrained on PPBenchand finetuned on two real-world applications: target-protein mini-binder complex design and antigen-antibody complex design. PPDiffconsistently surpasses baseline methods, achieving success rates of 50.00%, 23.16%, and 16.89% for the pretraining task and the two downstream applications, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PPDiff: Diffusing in Hybrid Sequence-Structure Space for Protein-Protein Complex Design":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PPDiff, a novel diffusion model aimed at jointly designing the sequence and structure of protein binders for arbitrary target proteins. It leverages a Sequence Structure Interleaving Network with Causal attention layers (SSINC) to capture global amino acid correlations, model local 3D interactions, and simplify dependencies within the protein sequence.  The model is pre-trained on a large curated dataset, PPBench, and then fine-tuned on tasks such as target-protein mini-binder complex design and antigen-antibody complex design. The authors show that PPDiff outperforms baseline methods in terms of success rate, novelty, and diversity in the designed complexes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the *joint* sequence and structure design of protein binders using a diffusion model with a specialized architecture (SSINC). While diffusion models have been applied to protein design previously (e.g., RFdiffusion), the simultaneous handling of discrete sequence information and continuous structural coordinates in the context of protein-protein *complex* design is a notable contribution. The introduction of causal attention layers within the SSINC architecture to simplify sequence dependencies is another aspect of novelty. However, several diffusion models have been proposed for protein design recently and SSINC incorporates known components, limiting novelty a bit.

* **Significance:** The ability to design high-affinity protein binders for arbitrary targets has broad applications in biomedicine and biotechnology. The potential of PPDiff to reduce the need for extensive wet-lab testing makes it a valuable tool.  The creation of PPBench, a large protein-protein complex dataset, is a significant contribution to the community, as it provides a resource for training and evaluating protein design models. While the results look promising and the novelty lies in the specific combination of methods and its successful application, without a experimental validation its immediate impact to the field will remain theoretical.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Joint Design:** Simultaneously modeling sequence and structure is a key strength, addressing common failure modes in binder design.
    * **SSINC Architecture:** The integration of self-attention, kNN graph convolution, and causal attention layers within SSINC appears well-suited to the protein design task.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors use a range of metrics (ipTM, pTM, PAE, PLDDT), including measures of novelty and diversity, to evaluate the designed complexes.
    * **Real-world Applications:** The evaluation on target-protein mini-binder and antigen-antibody design tasks demonstrates the practical relevance of PPDiff.
    * **PPBench Dataset:** The availability of PPBench facilitates further research in protein-protein complex design.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Lack of Experimental Validation:** The biggest weakness is the absence of experimental validation. While computational metrics are useful, demonstrating that the designed binders actually bind to their targets with the predicted affinity is crucial.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** PPDiff builds upon existing techniques in diffusion modeling and protein design.  While the combination is novel, the individual components are relatively well-established.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the ablation study of causal attention layer and diffusion steps is helpful, more extensive ablations could further clarify the contribution of each architectural component.
    * **Specialized nature:** The architecture seems designed for specific complex design, but no experimental validation is offered as to how to compare/differentiate PPDiff relative to other models for simple protein structure generation tasks.

* **Potential Influence:**  If PPDiff proves effective in experimental settings, it could become a widely used tool for protein binder design. The PPBench dataset will also likely be valuable for other researchers in the field. However, until experimental validation is presented, its impact on wet lab applications remains speculative.

* **Score Rationale:**
The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of protein design. The joint sequence-structure design approach and the introduction of the SSINC architecture are interesting. The creation of PPBench helps advance the field. However, the lack of experimental validation limits its immediate impact. While diffusion models and the SSINC architecture build upon existing work, the specific combination for the specific joint task presents some novelty. Overall the paper presents an interesting avenue for binder protein design, and is a potentially interesting and valuable improvement, but is not yet proven.

### Agent-RLVR: Training Software Engineering Agents via Guidance and Environment Rewards
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11425v1)
- **Authors**: Jeff Da, Clinton Wang, Xiang Deng, Yuntao Ma, Nikhil Barhate, Sean Hendryx
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) has been widely adopted as the de facto method for enhancing the reasoning capabilities of large language models and has demonstrated notable success in verifiable domains like math and competitive programming tasks. However, the efficacy of RLVR diminishes significantly when applied to agentic environments. These settings, characterized by multi-step, complex problem solving, lead to high failure rates even for frontier LLMs, as the reward landscape is too sparse for effective model training via conventional RLVR. In this work, we introduce Agent-RLVR, a framework that makes RLVR effective in challenging agentic settings, with an initial focus on software engineering tasks. Inspired by human pedagogy, Agent-RLVR introduces agent guidance, a mechanism that actively steers the agent towards successful trajectories by leveraging diverse informational cues. These cues, ranging from high-level strategic plans to dynamic feedback on the agent's errors and environmental interactions, emulate a teacher's guidance, enabling the agent to navigate difficult solution spaces and promotes active self-improvement via additional environment exploration. In the Agent-RLVR training loop, agents first attempt to solve tasks to produce initial trajectories, which are then validated by unit tests and supplemented with agent guidance. Agents then reattempt with guidance, and the agent policy is updated with RLVR based on the rewards of these guided trajectories. Agent-RLVR elevates the pass@1 performance of Qwen-2.5-72B-Instruct from 9.4% to 22.4% on SWE-Bench Verified. We find that our guidance-augmented RLVR data is additionally useful for test-time reward model training, shown by further boosting pass@1 to 27.8%. Agent-RLVR lays the groundwork for training agents with RLVR in complex, real-world environments where conventional RL methods struggle.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Agent-RLVR paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Agent-RLVR, a framework that enhances the effectiveness of Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) in complex agentic environments, particularly software engineering tasks. Recognizing that conventional RLVR struggles with sparse rewards and multi-step problem-solving in such environments, Agent-RLVR incorporates "agent guidance," a mechanism inspired by human pedagogy. This guidance actively steers the agent towards successful trajectories by providing diverse informational cues, including high-level strategic plans, feedback on errors, and environment interactions. The Agent-RLVR training loop involves initial attempts by the agent, validation through unit tests, generation of agent guidance for failed attempts, re-attempts with guidance, and policy updates via RLVR based on the rewards of guided trajectories. The authors curated a dataset of 817 software engineering environments with problem statements, environments, and guidance. Experimental results demonstrate that Agent-RLVR significantly improves the PASS@1 performance of a large language model (Qwen-2.5-72B-Instruct) on SWE-BENCH VERIFIED, and that the guidance-augmented RLVR data is also useful for test-time reward model training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the introduction and demonstration of "agent guidance" within an RLVR framework for complex, multi-step agentic tasks. While RLVR is established, its application to agentic environments has been limited due to the inherent challenges. Integrating guidance signals that mimic a teacher's input is a significant step towards addressing this limitation. The concept of leveraging different kinds of guidance (plan, environment feedback, interaction) is also a good aspect. The curated dataset of 817 environments with problem statements, environments, and guidance is also novel and useful.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to unlock the power of RLVR in challenging real-world environments where conventional RL methods often fail. The demonstrated success in software engineering, a practically relevant domain, is compelling. The ability to train agents capable of complex problem-solving through guidance and environment rewards opens doors for various applications, including automated code repair, system configuration, and robotic process automation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper accurately identifies the challenges of applying RLVR to agentic environments.
    *   Well-motivated approach: The "agent guidance" mechanism is inspired by human pedagogy and provides a practical way to address sparse rewards.
    *   Empirical validation: The experiments on SWE-BENCH VERIFIED demonstrate the effectiveness of Agent-RLVR and the usefulness of guidance.
    *   Useful Dataset: The curated dataset contains problem statements, environments, and guidance, enabling future work to be built upon.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Guidance Generation: The method for generating agent guidance depends on external LLM, which may result in issues of cost and also quality of generation.
    *   Limited Scope: The experiments are limited to a single domain (software engineering) and a single evaluation dataset (SWE-Bench VERIFIED). Generalizability to other agentic tasks needs to be further explored.
    *   Incremental Improvements: The baseline model is already performs well. The improvements may be incremental.
    *   Scalability: The results of the models scale linearly with model size, which may pose limitations in the future.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several directions:
    *   Development of more sophisticated guidance mechanisms for RLVR.
    *   Exploration of Agent-RLVR in other complex agentic environments.
    *   Integration of online RL techniques to further improve agent performance.
    *   Investigation of different reward functions and policy optimization algorithms within the Agent-RLVR framework.

*   **Rationale for Score:**

Given the novelty of the approach, the significance of the demonstrated results, and the potential influence on the field, but also considering the weaknesses of the paper, a score of 7 is warranted. The paper provides a solid foundation for future work, but further research is needed to address the limitations and fully realize the potential of Agent-RLVR.

Score: 7

- **Score**: 7/10

### KoGEC : Korean Grammatical Error Correction with Pre-trained Translation Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11432v1)
- **Authors**: Taeeun Kim, Semin Jeong, Youngsook Song
- **Abstract**: This research introduces KoGEC, a Korean Grammatical Error Correction system using pre\--trained translation models. We fine-tuned NLLB (No Language Left Behind) models for Korean GEC, comparing their performance against large language models like GPT-4 and HCX-3. The study used two social media conversation datasets for training and testing. The NLLB models were fine-tuned using special language tokens to distinguish between original and corrected Korean sentences. Evaluation was done using BLEU scores and an "LLM as judge" method to classify error types. Results showed that the fine-tuned NLLB (KoGEC) models outperformed GPT-4o and HCX-3 in Korean GEC tasks. KoGEC demonstrated a more balanced error correction profile across various error types, whereas the larger LLMs tended to focus less on punctuation errors. We also developed a Chrome extension to make the KoGEC system accessible to users. Finally, we explored token vocabulary expansion to further improve the model but found it to decrease model performance. This research contributes to the field of NLP by providing an efficient, specialized Korean GEC system and a new evaluation method. It also highlights the potential of compact, task-specific models to compete with larger, general-purpose language models in specialized NLP tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KOGEC, a Korean Grammatical Error Correction (GEC) system built using fine-tuned NLLB (No Language Left Behind) translation models. The authors compare KOGEC's performance against large language models like GPT-4 and HCX-3 on two social media conversation datasets. They fine-tune the NLLB models using special language tokens to differentiate between original and corrected Korean sentences. Evaluation is performed using BLEU scores and a novel "LLM as judge" method to classify error types. The results suggest that the fine-tuned NLLB models (KoGEC) outperform GPT-4o and HCX-3 in Korean GEC tasks, exhibiting a more balanced error correction profile. The authors also developed a Chrome extension to enhance accessibility and explored token vocabulary expansion, although the latter did not improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field of Korean GEC.  The novelty lies primarily in:

1.  **Application of NLLB to Korean GEC:** While previous work has explored NLLB for multilingual GEC, specifically tailoring it for Korean, with its unique grammatical characteristics, adds a specific contribution.
2.  **Comparative analysis:** The explicit comparison against strong baselines like GPT-4 and HCX-3 is valuable, particularly highlighting areas where the smaller, task-specific model can compete.
3.  **LLM as Judge for Error Type Classification:** The use of LLMs to categorize error types and analyze model performance beyond simple BLEU scores is a strong point. This provides more nuanced insight into the strengths and weaknesses of each approach.
4.  **Chrome extension:** Turning research into a usable tool.

However, the paper has weaknesses that need consideration:

*   **Dataset Details:** While the authors mention using two datasets and some preprocessing steps, more detailed information about the dataset creation, noise levels, and characteristics would be beneficial for reproducibility and understanding the results.
*   **Token Vocabulary Expansion Analysis:** While the result is negative, the discussion of the token vocabulary expansion experiment lacks depth. Why did it fail? What other strategies could be explored? A more detailed investigation is required.
*   **BLEU as a metric:** The authors are aware that BLEU is not perfect for GEC tasks as it measures similarity to a reference and not necessarily correctness. The reliance on BLEU as the primary metric, while acknowledging its limitations, is a minor concern.  The 'LLM as judge' evaluation partially addresses this.
*   **Error Type Classification Details:** The 'LLM as judge' method is interesting, but the implementation details (prompt, specific LLM used, error handling) could be elaborated upon. Inter-annotator agreement or validation of the LLM's classifications would strengthen the findings. While the study mentions visual validation by researchers, providing concrete examples or an assessment of the consistency and reliability of LLM judgments is crucial for bolstering confidence in this method. Without such validations, there's a significant risk of introducing biases or inconsistencies in the error type analysis, potentially skewing the evaluation results.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it demonstrates that, with careful fine-tuning, relatively compact, task-specific models can achieve strong results in a challenging NLP task like GEC, even when compared to much larger general-purpose models. The investigation of the “LLM as judge” method provides potential insight for future evaluation methodology.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates a practical application of NLLB to a specific language and provides a novel evaluation method. It shows the potential for smaller models to compete with large LLMs in specialized tasks. However, the lack of in-depth analysis and the limitations of the datasets and evaluation metrics hold it back from being a truly exceptional contribution. The absence of a thorough analysis of the dataset's characteristics, a deeper exploration of the token vocabulary expansion experiment, and more robust validation of the 'LLM as judge' method weaken the paper's overall impact.
Score: 7

### Auditing Data Provenance in Real-world Text-to-Image Diffusion Models for Privacy and Copyright Protection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11434v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Zhu, Leye Wang
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image diffusion model since its propose has significantly influenced the content creation due to its impressive generation capability. However, this capability depends on large-scale text-image datasets gathered from web platforms like social media, posing substantial challenges in copyright compliance and personal privacy leakage. Though there are some efforts devoted to explore approaches for auditing data provenance in text-to-image diffusion models, existing work has unrealistic assumptions that can obtain model internal knowledge, e.g., intermediate results, or the evaluation is not reliable. To fill this gap, we propose a completely black-box auditing framework called Feature Semantic Consistency-based Auditing (FSCA). It utilizes two types of semantic connections within the text-to-image diffusion model for auditing, eliminating the need for access to internal knowledge. To demonstrate the effectiveness of our FSCA framework, we perform extensive experiments on LAION-mi dataset and COCO dataset, and compare with eight state-of-the-art baseline approaches. The results show that FSCA surpasses previous baseline approaches across various metrics and different data distributions, showcasing the superiority of our FSCA. Moreover, we introduce a recall balance strategy and a threshold adjustment strategy, which collectively allows FSCA to reach up a user-level accuracy of 90% in a real-world auditing scenario with only 10 samples/user, highlighting its strong auditing potential in real-world applications. Our code is made available at https://github.com/JiePKU/FSCA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the critical issue of auditing data provenance in text-to-image diffusion models, specifically concerning privacy and copyright protection. Recognizing the limitations of existing approaches that often require unrealistic access to model internals or suffer from unreliable evaluations, the authors propose a novel, completely black-box auditing framework called Feature Semantic Consistency-based Auditing (FSCA). FSCA leverages semantic connections within text-image pairs created by diffusion models (specifically, the similarity between generated images and the text paired image, and alignment between the text description of the input text and the generated image) to determine if a particular text-image pair was used in the model's training.  The paper presents comprehensive experiments on LAION-mi and COCO datasets, comparing FSCA against various state-of-the-art baselines, demonstrating superior performance.  Crucially, the authors also introduce a recall balance strategy and a threshold adjustment strategy to enhance FSCA's performance in real-world user-level auditing scenarios, achieving high accuracy with limited data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in the design and implementation of a fully black-box auditing framework for text-to-image diffusion models. Unlike previous methods that often rely on gray-box assumptions (access to internal model parameters or intermediate outputs), FSCA operates solely on the input text and generated images, making it more practical for real-world applications. The use of semantic consistency between text and generated images as the core auditing principle is also a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly significant. The proliferation of text-to-image diffusion models raises serious concerns about copyright infringement and privacy violations due to their reliance on large-scale datasets scraped from the web. An effective auditing mechanism is essential for accountability and responsible AI development. The paper presents concrete results, demonstrating the superiority of the FSCA method over existing approaches, particularly in scenarios with limited user data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Black-Box Approach:** FSCA's black-box nature is a key strength, making it far more applicable than gray-box methods in real-world auditing.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The extensive experiments on two standard datasets (LAION-mi and COCO) provide robust evidence for the effectiveness and generalizability of FSCA.
    *   **Real-world Scenarios:** The user-level auditing scenario and the recall balance/threshold adjustment strategies directly address practical challenges in deploying such systems.
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-written, clearly articulating the problem, the proposed solution, and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Encoders:** FSCA relies on pre-trained multimodal encoders (CLIP, specifically mentioned in the text). The performance of FSCA is intrinsically linked to the quality and biases present in these encoders, and the choice of encoders may affect the outcomes. The dependence on CLIP, while practical, could be seen as limiting the novelty of the method itself. An ablation study examining different pre-trained encoders or more discussion on how to mitigate biases in these encoders would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides intuitive explanations for the effectiveness of the proposed techniques (semantic consistency, recall balance, threshold adjustment), but lacks a more rigorous theoretical analysis.
    *  **Computational Cost Considerations:** While a strength is that no internal access is required of the diffusion model, repeatedly generating N images with the diffusion model for each data point to audit can be computationally expensive. A discussion of computational costs or how the method can be made more efficient would be helpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's practical approach and promising results have the potential to influence the development of auditing tools for text-to-image diffusion models. It could encourage further research into black-box auditing techniques and the development of more robust privacy and copyright protection mechanisms.

*   **Justification:** The development of FSCA and experiments on the dataset clearly illustrate how auditing can be made fully black-box. Overall, while improvements are desirable to broaden the framework's theoretical grounding and practical scalability, this research contributes with valuable, practical insights.

**Score: 7.5**

Rationale:

The paper offers a significant advance in the practical auditing of text-to-image diffusion models.  The black-box nature and comprehensive evaluation of FSCA clearly address a gap in the existing literature. The user-level auditing experiments add considerable practical value.  The reliance on pre-trained encoders is a moderate limitation, and further work could be performed on theoretical foundation and computational cost. Taking these factors into account, a score of 7.5 reflects the paper's meaningful contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AbsenceBench: Language Models Can't Tell What's Missing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11440v1)
- **Authors**: Harvey Yiyun Fu, Aryan Shrivastava, Jared Moore, Peter West, Chenhao Tan, Ari Holtzman
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly capable of processing long inputs and locating specific information within them, as evidenced by their performance on the Needle in a Haystack (NIAH) test. However, while models excel at recalling surprising information, they still struggle to identify clearly omitted information. We introduce AbsenceBench to assesses LLMs' capacity to detect missing information across three domains: numerical sequences, poetry, and GitHub pull requests. AbsenceBench asks models to identify which pieces of a document were deliberately removed, given access to both the original and edited contexts. Despite the apparent straightforwardness of these tasks, our experiments reveal that even state-of-the-art models like Claude-3.7-Sonnet achieve only 69.6% F1-score with a modest average context length of 5K tokens. Our analysis suggests this poor performance stems from a fundamental limitation: Transformer attention mechanisms cannot easily attend to "gaps" in documents since these absences don't correspond to any specific keys that can be attended to. Overall, our results and analysis provide a case study of the close proximity of tasks where models are already superhuman (NIAH) and tasks where models breakdown unexpectedly (AbsenceBench).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AbsenceBench: Language Models Can't Tell What's Missing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AbsenceBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate large language models' (LLMs) ability to identify conspicuously missing information from long inputs. In contrast to the Needle-in-a-Haystack (NIAH) test, which assesses LLMs' capacity to locate small bits of surprising information within large contexts, AbsenceBench prompts LLMs to identify intentionally omitted information.  The benchmark comprises three domains: poetry, numerical sequences, and GitHub pull requests.  The authors evaluated a variety of LLMs, including state-of-the-art closed-source and open-source models.  The results revealed that even cutting-edge models perform surprisingly poorly on AbsenceBench, despite the simplicity of the task and its apparent similarity to the NIAH test.  The paper also investigates the effects of context length, omission rate, and inference-time compute on performance. The authors hypothesize that Transformer attention mechanisms struggle with absences, as there are no specific keys to attend to in those regions. They show that explicitly marking omissions significantly improves performance, suggesting a potential weakness in the Transformer's self-attention mechanism.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of testing LLMs' ability to detect the *absence* of information, rather than its presence, is a novel and potentially important one. The contrast with the NIAH benchmark is insightful, highlighting a blind spot in LLM evaluation and, perhaps, in LLM architecture itself. The construction of AbsenceBench across different domains (poetry, numerical sequences, GitHub PRs) is a positive aspect, adding to the generalizability of the findings. However, the individual tasks within each domain are not *extremely* novel task formulations.

**Significance:** The findings have significant implications for understanding the limitations of LLMs and the potential shortcomings of current evaluation metrics. If LLMs struggle to identify missing information, it raises concerns about their reliability in applications where this ability is crucial, such as LLM-as-a-judge, misinformation detection, or any scenario requiring comprehensive analysis. The result of explicit placeholder injection as a performance booster is also important, directly pointing to transformer model limitations. However, the benchmark only captures *surface-level* absences. Semantic or inferential absences are not considered. Furthermore, the study only uses prompts and does not explore fine-tuning or other techniques to improve absence detection.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of absence detection and its importance.
*   **Well-Designed Benchmark:** The construction of the AbsenceBench benchmark is commendable, covering a range of domains and context lengths.
*   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of factors affecting performance (context length, omission rate, inference-time compute) is insightful and helps to identify potential causes for the observed limitations.
*   **Compelling Hypothesis:** The hypothesis that Transformer attention mechanisms struggle with absences is intriguing and supported by the experimental results.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper highlights the practical implications of these findings for real-world applications of LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The benchmark only considers surface-level absences, which may not be representative of all types of missing information in real-world scenarios. It doesn't venture into complex semantic or reasoning-based absences.
*   **Surface Level Prompts and Training:** The authors only explore a few prompt variations.  A more systematic exploration of prompt engineering or fine-tuning strategies could reveal ways to improve performance on AbsenceBench. Moreover, it relies solely on prompting.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Depth:** While the paper presents a compelling hypothesis about Transformer attention, it lacks a more formal or theoretical analysis of why this limitation arises.
*   **No Baselines Using Non-LLM Methods:** A performance comparison with non-LLM information retrieval or traditional NLP methods might provide a valuable perspective on the difficulty of the task and the specific limitations of LLMs.
*   **Statistical Significance:** The lack of statistical significance testing due to cost constraints is a minor concern.
*   **Limited Omission Strategies:** The paper only omits parts of the sequence. The effect of replacing the sequence with garbage is unexplored.

**Potential Influence:** The paper could significantly influence the field by:

*   Motivating the development of new evaluation metrics that explicitly assess LLMs' ability to detect missing information.
*   Encouraging research into architectural modifications or training techniques that improve absence detection capabilities.
*   Raising awareness about the limitations of LLMs in applications where identifying missing information is critical.
*   Expanding the conceptual framework of "hallucinations" to include weak identification of absences as part of a broader model weakness.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and important problem, supported by a well-designed benchmark and insightful analysis. The implications for LLM evaluation and application are significant. However, the limited scope (surface-level absences), prompt variations, lack of statistical robustness, and the absence of deeper theoretical analysis or non-LLM baselines detract from the overall impact.  The paper serves as an important starting point, highlighting a crucial area for future research, but requires more in-depth investigation to reach its full potential and therefore it does not receive a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReVeal: Self-Evolving Code Agents via Iterative Generation-Verification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11442v1)
- **Authors**: Yiyang Jin, Kunzhao Xu, Hang Li, Xueting Han, Yanmin Zhou, Cheng Li, Jing Bai
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable outcome rewards have significantly improved the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), especially when combined with multi-turn tool interactions. However, existing methods lack both meaningful verification signals from realistic environments and explicit optimization for verification, leading to unreliable self-verification. To address these limitations, we propose ReVeal, a multi-turn reinforcement learning framework that interleaves code generation with explicit self-verification and tool-based evaluation. ReVeal enables LLMs to autonomously generate test cases, invoke external tools for precise feedback, and improves performance via a customized RL algorithm with dense, per-turn rewards. As a result, ReVeal fosters the co-evolution of a model's generation and verification capabilities through RL training, expanding the reasoning boundaries of the base model, demonstrated by significant gains in Pass@k on LiveCodeBench. It also enables test-time scaling into deeper inference regimes, with code consistently evolving as the number of turns increases during inference, ultimately surpassing DeepSeek-R1-Zero-Qwen-32B. These findings highlight the promise of ReVeal as a scalable and effective paradigm for building more robust and autonomous AI agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ReVeal: Self-Evolving Code Agents via Iterative Generation-Verification":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReVeal, a novel multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to improve the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), particularly in code generation. ReVeal differs from existing RL approaches by interleaving code generation with explicit self-verification using external tools and a customized RL algorithm. This allows the LLM to generate test cases, obtain feedback, and iteratively refine its code, leading to a co-evolution of its generation and verification capabilities.  The core ideas of ReVeal are iterative generation-verification, where the model explicitly constructs test cases and validates code correctness, and dense, per-turn rewards to facilitate better optimization compared to sparse outcome rewards in standard RL. The framework also features mechanisms to ensure robustness and prevent adversarial reward gaming. Experiments on LiveCodeBench demonstrate significant gains in code accuracy (Pass@k) and an ability to scale inference into deeper reasoning regimes, outperforming base models and standard RL baselines, and even exceeding the performance of a larger model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits substantial novelty. While the idea of using RL for code generation and incorporating external tools isn't entirely new, ReVeal's unique contribution lies in:

    *   **Explicit Iterative Self-Verification:** ReVeal's explicit design to iterate between generation and verification with tool interactions at each step. The explicit, structured approach to self-verification distinguishes it from implicit methods or single-turn RL.
    *   **Dense Turn-Level Rewards:** The framework uses dense rewards at each generation-verification turn, enabling more granular optimization compared to relying only on the final outcome. This allows for more effective optimization of both code quality and verification accuracy.
    *   **Robustness Mechanisms:** Addressing potential adversarial reward gaming through mechanisms to maintain robustness in test case generation.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from:

    *   **Improved Performance:** Demonstrating tangible and statistically significant performance gains on a challenging code benchmark (LiveCodeBench) compared to strong baselines. The increase in pass rate and the ability to outperform a larger model (DeepSeek-R1) are compelling.
    *   **Test-Time Scaling:** The framework's ability to scale into deeper inference regimes is particularly important. This suggests ReVeal could unlock better performance with increasing compute without retraining, which is highly desirable for practical applications.
    *   **Co-evolution of Capabilities:**  The concept of co-evolving generation and verification capabilities is novel and promising for building more robust AI agents. This opens up interesting research directions.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The framework is relatively straightforward to implement and can be potentially applied to various domains where iterative refinement and verification are crucial.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** ReVeal is a well-defined framework with clear components and a structured approach.
    *   **Strong Experimental Validation:** The experiments are thorough, using a challenging benchmark and comparing against multiple baselines.  The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components.
    *   **Clear Results and Analysis:** The paper presents the results clearly and provides a good analysis of the findings.
    *   **Addresses Key Limitations:** The work directly addresses the limitations of existing methods by explicitly optimizing for verification and providing meaningful feedback.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The performance is demonstrated on a specific coding benchmark (LiveCodeBench). Further evaluations on more diverse and real-world code generation tasks would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Scalability Considerations:** While the paper demonstrates test-time scaling, the computational cost of running multiple turns of generation and verification, especially with external tools, could be a limiting factor for larger-scale applications. This aspect could be discussed in greater detail.
    *   **Overfitting to Reward Structure:** Like all RL-based approaches, ReVeal is sensitive to the design of the reward function. The paper discusses strategies to mitigate adversarial reward gaming, but there could still be potential for the model to learn suboptimal strategies that maximize reward but don't generalize well.

*   **Potential Influence:** ReVeal has the potential to influence future research in:

    *   **Self-improving AI Agents:** ReVeal serves as a promising blueprint for building more autonomous, self-improving AI agents that can iteratively refine their performance.
    *   **Tool-Integrated Reasoning:** The work can encourage the development of more sophisticated tool-integrated reasoning frameworks.
    *   **RL for Code Generation:**  ReVeal could become a foundational method that spurs future advancements in RL-based code generation.

*   **Justification of Score:** ReVeal presents a clear advancement in the field of RL for code generation. The core ideas are novel, well-validated, and have the potential to impact how we build self-improving AI agents. While there are areas for further exploration and validation, the demonstrated performance gains, test-time scaling capability, and co-evolution concept warrant a high score.
    A score of 8 reflects a high level of novelty, significance, and potential impact, acknowledging that further research and broad application validations would solidify the framework.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### GaussMarker: Robust Dual-Domain Watermark for Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11444v1)
- **Authors**: Kecen Li, Zhicong Huang, Xinwen Hou, Cheng Hong
- **Abstract**: As Diffusion Models (DM) generate increasingly realistic images, related issues such as copyright and misuse have become a growing concern. Watermarking is one of the promising solutions. Existing methods inject the watermark into the single-domain of initial Gaussian noise for generation, which suffers from unsatisfactory robustness. This paper presents the first dual-domain DM watermarking approach using a pipelined injector to consistently embed watermarks in both the spatial and frequency domains. To further boost robustness against certain image manipulations and advanced attacks, we introduce a model-independent learnable Gaussian Noise Restorer (GNR) to refine Gaussian noise extracted from manipulated images and enhance detection robustness by integrating the detection scores of both watermarks. GaussMarker efficiently achieves state-of-the-art performance under eight image distortions and four advanced attacks across three versions of Stable Diffusion with better recall and lower false positive rates, as preferred in real applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "GaussMarker: Robust Dual-Domain Watermark for Diffusion Models" introduces a new watermarking technique for diffusion models (DMs), addressing the challenge of copyright protection and misuse. Unlike existing methods that embed watermarks in a single domain (spatial or frequency) of the initial Gaussian noise, GaussMarker employs a "dual-domain" approach, embedding watermarks consistently in both the spatial and frequency domains using a pipelined injector. Furthermore, the paper proposes a model-independent learnable Gaussian Noise Restorer (GNR) to refine Gaussian noise extracted from manipulated images, enhancing detection robustness against image distortions and advanced attacks. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across various image distortions and attacks on three versions of Stable Diffusion.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel dual-domain watermarking technique for diffusion models. The combined spatial and frequency domain embedding is a well-reasoned approach, drawing inspiration from traditional image watermarking and adapting it to the unique characteristics of diffusion models.  The GNR is another significant addition, addressing the robustness issues that plague existing tuning-free methods, especially against rotation and cropping. The model independence of GNR is a plus, but it also highlights some limitations.

* **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in addressing a critical gap in the practical applicability of DM watermarking. Existing tuning-free methods, while convenient, often lack robustness against common image manipulations, hindering their use in real-world scenarios. GaussMarker significantly improves the detection rate under image distortions and advanced attacks, bringing watermarking closer to being a viable solution for copyright protection and misuse prevention in the DM space. The demonstration on multiple Stable Diffusion versions enhances its practical relevance. The relatively small footprint of GNR also adds to its significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Dual-Domain Approach:** The core idea of combining spatial and frequency domain watermarking is well-motivated and empirically validated.
    * **Gaussian Noise Restorer (GNR):** The GNR is a smart approach to enhance robustness, decoupling the watermarking method from the specific architecture.  Its model-independence is a clear strength.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation, covering a wide range of image distortions and advanced attacks, across multiple Stable Diffusion versions. The comparisons to state-of-the-art methods are extensive.
    * **Performance:** GaussMarker achieves state-of-the-art performance with high true positive rate (TPR) and low false positive rate (FPR).

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on DDIM Inversion:** Similar to other tuning-free methods, the dependency on DDIM inversion to estimate the Gaussian noise map may limit its applicability in scenarios where ODE solvers are preferred or the underlying sampler is unknown. A detailed examination of performance under other inversion methods would be beneficial.
    * **GNR Limitations:** While GNR significantly enhances robustness, it might introduce artifacts if the watermarked image has undergone excessive manipulations (beyond the training scenarios) – this trade-off is not deeply analyzed. Its model-independence highlights that GNR cannot make use of the internal details of the different diffusion model types, limiting GNR's potential performance.
    * **FID and CLIP Score Tradeoff:** The FID and CLIP scores are good, but the performance of GaussMarker does come with a potential tradeoff on the FID. Future work could investigate approaches to reduce artifacts to minimize FID changes.
    * **Zero-bit Frequency Watermark**: While explained in Appendix B.2, the choice of a zero-bit watermark in the frequency domain should have a greater justification. Also, in future iterations, the zero-bit watermark can be revisited.

* **Potential Impact:** The proposed technique has the potential to become a standard approach for watermarking diffusion models. The dual-domain approach, combined with the GNR, offers a tangible improvement in robustness, making DM watermarking more practical. It provides a solid foundation for future research in the area.

**Justification of Score**

I am assigning a score of **8** to this paper.

*Rationale:*

The paper's novelty is solid. The dual-domain approach and GNR are both innovative contributions. The comprehensive experimental validation demonstrates the practical significance and substantial improvements over existing techniques. However, the limitations regarding DDIM inversion, FID degradation, and potentially limited GNR performance restrict the overall score. The lack of further exploration around the zero-bit frequency watermark also holds back the score. While these weaknesses exist, the core ideas presented are strong and the empirical results are compelling, warranting a high score. The practical focus and improvements in robustness make it a valuable contribution.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Leveraging Reference Documents for Zero-Shot Ranking via Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11452v1)
- **Authors**: Jieran Li, Xiuyuan Hu, Yang Zhao, Shengyao Zhuang, Hao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated exceptional performance in the task of text ranking for information retrieval. While Pointwise ranking approaches offer computational efficiency by scoring documents independently, they often yield biased relevance estimates due to the lack of inter-document comparisons. In contrast, Pairwise methods improve ranking accuracy by explicitly comparing document pairs, but suffer from substantial computational overhead with quadratic complexity ($O(n^2)$). To address this tradeoff, we propose \textbf{RefRank}, a simple and effective comparative ranking method based on a fixed reference document. Instead of comparing all document pairs, RefRank prompts the LLM to evaluate each candidate relative to a shared reference anchor. By selecting the reference anchor that encapsulates the core query intent, RefRank implicitly captures relevance cues, enabling indirect comparison between documents via this common anchor. This reduces computational cost to linear time ($O(n)$) while importantly, preserving the advantages of comparative evaluation. To further enhance robustness, we aggregate multiple RefRank outputs using a weighted averaging scheme across different reference choices. Experiments on several benchmark datasets and with various LLMs show that RefRank significantly outperforms Pointwise baselines and could achieve performance at least on par with Pairwise approaches with a significantly lower computational cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RefRank, a novel approach to zero-shot document ranking using Large Language Models (LLMs).  RefRank addresses the trade-off between the efficiency of Pointwise ranking and the accuracy of Pairwise ranking. It does this by using a single (or multiple) reference document(s) to which all candidate documents are compared. This allows for indirect comparisons between documents via the shared reference, reducing the computational complexity to linear time O(n) while preserving the advantages of comparative evaluation. The paper explores different strategies for selecting reference documents (using top-ranked documents from an initial BM25 retrieval) and demonstrates the effectiveness of RefRank through experiments across several benchmark datasets and with various LLMs, showing significant improvements over Pointwise baselines and comparable performance to Pairwise approaches at a lower computational cost.  The method uses weighted averaging of multiple reference documents to further enhance robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using a *reference document* for ranking is interesting and provides a balanced approach between Pointwise and Pairwise ranking strategies. The novelty lies in the clever adaptation of the "control group" concept from scientific experiments to the ranking problem, enabling indirect comparisons without the O(n^2) complexity of explicit pairwise comparisons. The exploration of using top-ranked documents from a preliminary retrieval phase to select the reference documents is also a novel and practical touch, drawing on ideas from pseudo-relevance feedback.  The weighted averaging of multiple reference documents adds another layer of sophistication.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in LLM-based ranking: the computational cost of pairwise methods. RefRank provides a computationally efficient alternative that can achieve comparable or even better results than pairwise methods. This is particularly important for real-world applications where scalability is crucial. The experimental results demonstrate the practical value of the approach across different datasets and models.  The paper's findings concerning the optimal number of reference documents (top-2) and weights (limited sequential weighting) provide useful guidelines for practitioners.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly identifies the trade-offs in existing ranking approaches and motivates the need for a more balanced solution.
    * **Well-defined method:** RefRank is presented as a simple yet effective method with a clear algorithmic description.
    * **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper includes thorough experiments on several benchmark datasets with different LLMs, demonstrating the robustness of the approach. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different components of RefRank.
    * **Solid empirical validation:** The results consistently show that RefRank outperforms Pointwise methods and is competitive with Pairwise approaches at a significantly lower computational cost.
    * **Careful analysis:**  The paper provides a detailed analysis of the experimental results, including insights into the selection of reference documents and weighting strategies.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on initial retrieval stage:** The quality of RefRank depends on the initial BM25 retrieval. This makes RefRank somewhat of a "re-ranker" and doesn't completely eliminate the dependence on traditional retrieval methods. However, this is explicitly acknowledged.
    * **Limited exploration of reference document selection:** While the paper explores selecting reference documents from the top-k retrieved documents, it does not consider more sophisticated methods, for example, document clustering and selecting the centroid as reference documents or using a retrieval model to specifically find diverse reference documents.
    * **Theoretical analysis:** While the paper discusses the computational complexity, a more in-depth theoretical analysis of the properties of the method (e.g., convergence, bias) would strengthen the paper.
    * **Generalization to other tasks:** The experiments are focused on document ranking. It would be beneficial to investigate the applicability of RefRank to other information retrieval or NLP tasks (e.g., summarization, question answering).

* **Potential Influence:** RefRank provides a pragmatic and efficient solution to the ranking problem with LLMs. It's likely to influence future research on LLM-based ranking by providing a solid baseline and a starting point for further improvements. The idea of using reference documents could be extended to other tasks involving comparative evaluation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM-based ranking. While the limitations related to dependence on initial retrieval are present, the method addresses a core problem – the computational cost of pairwise ranking – and achieves compelling results. The research is well-motivated, the method is clearly explained, and the experiments are thorough and well-analyzed. It presents a clever solution and provides insight to other researchers in the area.  While a more in-depth theoretical analysis and broader investigation of alternative reference selection could be done, the overall contribution and practicality of the solution warrant a high score.
